| **`compute --write` ignored imported repositories.** It rediscovered packages from the control config and root instead of the composed workspace, so an imports-only run exited successfully without scanning source manifests, deriving cross-source edges, or writing the consumer owner's config and backup. | `TestPolyrepoComputeWritesImportedOwnerConfig` | `polyrepo_test.go`; `internal/app` |
| **`dispat compute` could not repair a one-sided fleet link.** The docs and the saga's own findings say `W332` is what the command repairs, but the link set was derived from a union-find seeded with the links that exist: once one end declared the link the pair counted as joined, so the missing half was never proposed and a run interrupted between the two halves could only be finished by hand. | `TestChoreographyComputeDeclaresTheHalfOfAOneSidedLink`, `TestChoreographyComputeWithholdsAOneSidedHalfWithoutARemote`, `TestChoreographyFaultStopsComputeDeclaringTheOtherHalf`; `services/dispat/internal/app/computelinks_test.go::TestSuggestLinksProposesTheHalfOfAOneSidedLink` | `choreography_compute_test.go`, `choreography_faults_test.go`; `internal/app` |
| **A release planned from the local clone's tags although it held the lock.** The lock precedes planning and the plan reads release tags from the checkout, so a clone made before another run recorded re-planned the published version, published it again and pushed its own tag over the record; with `--no-tags` at a newer head it moved the published tag. Reproduced on the shipped binary. | `TestReleaseRecordsRefuseACloneWithoutTheRemoteTags`, `TestReleaseRecordsNeverMoveAPublishedTag`, `TestReleaseRecordsInAComposedWorkspace`; `services/dispat/internal/app/release_records_test.go::TestReleaseStoreComparison` | `release_records_test.go`; `internal/app` |
| **A release tag was force-pushed with the rest of the run's refs.** `commit.force` defaults on and the push forced every tag it carried, so a record the remote already held at another commit was replaced rather than reported, and `CommitConfig.Force` promised the opposite. | `TestReleaseRecordsCreateOnlyPush`, `TestReleaseRecordsAliasTagsStillMove`; `services/dispat/internal/gitx/gitx_test.go::TestPushMovesOnlyTheRefsDeclaredMoving` | `release_records_test.go`; `internal/gitx` |
| **A fleet change `compute --write` could not make failed the run silently.** Every other refusal in that command reports itself before returning and the dispatcher only turns an error into an exit status, so a link that could not be created, declared or pinned left the operator with exit 1, no diagnostic, and no sentence naming the repository, the peer or the cause. | `TestChoreographyFaultStopsComputeAndConvergesOnASecondRun`, `TestChoreographyFaultStopsComputeDeclaringTheOtherHalf`, `TestChoreographyFaultStopsComputeInitializingADeclaredLink` | `choreography_faults_test.go`; `internal/app` |
| **A stale worker state lock could admit two serving processes.** Renaming a stale PID file aside created an absent-path window: a second claimant could create its own lock while the first owner kept serving. Every claim now settles for the write grace and is trusted only if the PID file still names its process, and a serving worker reads the file again before each claim and stops when another process's id is there. | `TestExecutionWorkerConcurrentStaleStateClaimHasOneOwner`, `TestExecutionWorkerStopsWhenAnotherProcessClaimsItsFolder`, `TestExecutionWorkerStartRefusals` | `execution_worker_test.go`; `internal/execution` `TestNodeStateClaimSettlesBeforeItIsTrusted`, `TestAStaleLockIsTakenOverWithoutTakingAFreshOne`, `TestWorkerStopsWhenAnotherProcessClaimsItsFolder` |
| **The publication order was taken over the subgraph the plan induces.** A consumer and a provider it reaches only through a package with nothing to release had no edge between them, so the two publishes were mutually unordered and the consumer could publish first, carrying a range reconciled against a version nobody had published. A package with no bump this run is the commonest shape an incremental release has. | `TestPublishOrderHoldsThroughAPackageThatIsNotReleasing`; `TestPublishOrderHoldsThroughAPackageThatIsNotInThePlan`, `TestPublishOrderReachesThroughAPackageThatIsNotInThePlan`, `TestPublishOrderConvergesThroughTwoUnreleasingPackages` | `publish_order_test.go`; `internal/release` |
| **A mailbox repacked itself while the scenario that made it was being cleaned up.** `git receive-pack` starts a detached repack after a push, and the bare repository standing in for a mailbox lives in a folder the test framework removes when the scenario ends: the repack went on writing objects into it during the removal and failed scenarios for a reason unrelated to what they asserted. Housekeeping is switched off in every mailbox the suite creates. | `TestExecutionCraftedOutputSetsNeverReachAConsumer` (seen failing once in three runs; green in three after) | `execution_authority_test.go` |
| **One failed read of a coordination branch cost a whole task deadline.** The poll memo is recorded as soon as a branch's objects are fetched, before the caller has made anything of them, so a local git failure while resolving the chain or reading the message counted as having dealt with the branch. A tip that never moved again was never offered a second time, and the run waited out `timeouts.task` for an answer sitting in the mailbox. Both poll loops now reconsider such a branch, while a message the protocol refuses is still not re-read. | `TestExecutionOrchestratorGitFaults` (row "the chain cannot be walked once"); `services/dispat/internal/execution/worker_test.go::TestWorkerReadsABranchAgainWhenReadingItFailed` | `execution_faults_test.go`; `internal/execution` |
| **A delegated build of a package a peer owns could not be given a checkout.** A node materializes one worktree per repository of the package's input closure, and the closure named the repository owning the package first. In a linked fleet that is not the repository the others sit inside, so the nested checkout was created first and the entry's own worktree was then refused the folder the nested one had just made: every such build failed with E227 before a command ran. | `TestExecutionModesReleaseAsTheirTwinWithoutWorkers` (row "linked peers"), `TestExecutionFleetBuildSeesProviderOutputsAcrossRepositories`; `services/dispat/internal/app/execution_dispatch_test.go::TestAnInputClosureIsMaterializedOutsideIn` | `execution_modes_test.go`; `internal/app` |
| **A failed provider blocked its declared consumers only.** The W194 closure read the direct providers of a package, so a provider that failed left every consumer behind an unreleasing package free to publish against a version that never went out. The closure is the same one the order is taken over. | `TestPublishOrderBlocksAConsumerBehindAFailedProviderItDoesNotNameDirectly`; `TestPublishOrderBlocksAConsumerBehindAReachedProvider`, `TestPublishOrderSkipsAConsumerItReachesOnlyThroughAnUnreleasingPackage` | `publish_order_test.go`; `internal/release` |
| **A consumer that proceeded past a failed provider was orphaned.** The bump axis admitted a unit for a dependent only while the dependent's own pending window held the commit, so a consumer that released on a fresh bump of its own while its provider's publish failed carried the commit behind its own baseline: it was never planned again, kept the provider's previous version for ever, and no diagnostic fired. Admission now follows delivery. | `TestAdmissionCatchesUpAConsumerThatProceededOnItsOwn`, `TestAdmissionCatchesUpAConsumerThatOvertookAHeldProvider`; `services/dispat/internal/plan/admission_test.go::TestAdmissionCatchesUpAConsumerThatOvertookItsProvider`, `TestAdmissionLeavesHistoriesWithoutOvertakingUnchanged` | `admission_test.go`; `internal/plan` |
| **A consumer that proceeded published a manifest naming a version nobody published.** Its version stage wrote each provider's planned version while the provider was still going to publish it, and a provider that died afterwards left the range pointing at a version that will never exist. The reconciliation is redone against the live providers before the publish, and a consumer whose build already embedded the planned version is blocked instead. | `TestAdmissionCatchesUpAConsumerThatProceededOnItsOwn` (row "the consumer proceeds naming the provider's baseline"), `TestAdmissionBlocksAProceedingConsumerWhoseBuildEmbeddedThePlannedVersion`; `services/dispat/internal/release/admission_test.go::TestAdmissionFindsTheProvidersThatDiedAfterTheVersionStage` | `admission_test.go`; `internal/release` |
| A publishing node that refused an authorization, a withdrawal or a stray message leased its failed result over the ready commit although the branch had moved past it, so the result could never be written, the run waited out its task deadline and, for an authorization it had written itself (for example one the node read after its expiry), reported an outcome it could not establish and retained the release lock for nothing. | `TestExecutionCraftedPublicationAuthorizations` (every row asserts the failed result on top of the refused message) | `tests/integration/execution_authorization_test.go` |
| A withdrawal the run wrote on top of an authorization the node had not read yet was refused as a replay, because the node compared it with the ready commit it was waiting at, so an interrupted run left the attempt unanswered and called a command that never started an unknown outcome. | `TestExecutionCraftedPublicationWithdrawals` ("a withdrawal written on top of an authorization the node has not read is acknowledged") | `tests/integration/execution_authorization_test.go` |
| Build outputs were captured with `git add`, which applies the checkout's attributes to whatever it stages: a `text` attribute rewrote every CRLF pair inside three libraries of a project that marks its tree as text, and the consumers installed corrupted binaries that the project's own integrity check refused. The forced capture now hashes every file with no filter and fills the index by hand. | `TestExecutionOutputBytesIgnoreTheCheckoutAttributes`; unit `TestTransportPlumbingCapturesBytesUntouchedByAttributes` | `tests/integration/execution_outputs_test.go`, `services/dispat/internal/gitx/transport_test.go` |
| **Two peers' conflicting boundaries were merged silently.** Merging the fleet's `repositoryBaselines` kept the first tuple for a consumer, release tag and repository and dropped any later one unread, so the E333 duplicate checks never saw a peer whose revision differed: a run from one peer planned a catch-up and a run from the other planned nothing. Every peer's tuple is resolved, one commit stated twice is kept once, and two commits are E333 naming both peers. | `TestChoreographyRefusesConflictingBoundariesTwoPeersState`; `services/dispat/internal/config/workspace_choreography_test.go::TestChoreographyRefusesConflictingBaselinesFromTwoPeers` | `choreography_compose_test.go`; `internal/config` |

## Running

Integration coverage is gathered from the instrumented CLI build that goal 49 guards, merged with the profile of the
public-API test binaries that goal 61 drives. The suite runs from its own module:

```sh
cd tests/integration
go test ./...            # requires git and the go toolchain on PATH
go test ./... -race      # also clean
```

dispat builds binaries once per `go test` invocation and shares them across all tests. Each test creates its repository
in a fresh `t.TempDir()`, keeping tests isolated and safe to run in any order or subset.

## Conventions for new tests

- Assert against JSON events (`res.Events`, `harness.IsCodePresentForPackage`) and git state, never against pretty log
  text. Prefer `IsCodePresentForPackage` over `IsCodePresent` whenever the diagnostic names a package.
- Author configs as `pkg/models` values starting from `harness.BaseFile(concurrency...)` and write them with
  `r.WriteConfigModel(cfg)`. Fall back to `harness.WriteConfigRaw` only for shapes the model cannot express, such as
  unknown keys. `polyrepo_test.go` deliberately authors raw JSON while the new composition schema settles, keeping
  repository ownership and import boundaries visible in each fixture. Reuse shared fixtures in `helpers_test.go` (`singlePackageRepo`, `linkedRepo`, `libsConfig`,
  `markerBuild`/`buildRuns` for script-execution claims). If only one test uses a config, write it out fully inside
  that test to keep the input visible.
- Use `r.ReleaseOK()` and `r.StatusOK()` for runs that must succeed. Use plain `Release()` or `Status()` with an
  explicit code assertion when you expect a non-zero exit code.
- `HasTag` requires an exact match. Use `TagCount("pkg@")` to check whether a package was tagged at all, because an
  exact match against a bare prefix passes vacuously.
- Use `tsmark` for assertions about *when* something ran. Use `AssertSequential` for flake-free claims about execution
  *order*. Reserve `AssertOverlaps` and `AssertConcurrencyBudget` with generous sleeps for tests that require actual
  concurrency.
- Build one multi-run scenario per behaviour cluster. Adding extra runs to an existing fixture is faster than creating
  a new one, and testing convergence (running again changes nothing) validates stability.
- `harness.BaseFile` disables GitHub by default. Re-enable it only when testing recorders by overriding `GitHub`
  against an httptest server.
